284. **The PreToolUse hook denies an unnamed klatch command from an in-process subagent (#1152; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** A live run (2026-11-21, headless Claude Code) showed that a subagent the harness runs inside the session's own process cannot be told from its parent by anything its command can see: the environment has no agent id and the process chain is the parent's. The one signal is the hook input: the PreToolUse and PostToolUse of a subagent's tool call carry `agent_id` and `agent_type`, and the parent's own calls carry neither. klatch's PreToolUse hook already reads `agent_id` (#505), so the fix lives there and keeps the decision of #503 (a subagent that posts must name itself). Rule: for a `Bash` call whose hook input has a non-empty `agent_id`, if the command invokes klatch and sets no name for itself, the hook exits 2 with a message on stderr: the command runs inside a subagent and does not name itself, set `KLATCH_NAME` to a name for this subagent (for example `KLATCH_NAME=<task>-1`) in front of the klatch command and run it again. A command that does not invoke klatch, a command that names itself, a call from the parent, a call with an empty `agent_id` and a tool that is not `Bash` are untouched. Reading the command text: it is data, tokenized and never run (`domain::invokes_klatch_unnamed`). Quotes group a word and hide separators; an unquoted `;`, `&`, `|`, newline, parenthesis or backtick ends a simple command. In each simple command leading `NAME=value` words, wrappers (`env`, `command`, `exec`, `time`, `nohup`, `nice`, `sudo`, `builtin`, `xargs`, `!`) and flags are skipped, and the command word counts as klatch when its basename is `klatch`. `sh|bash|zsh|dash|ksh -c '<text>'` is read the same way, to a depth of four. So `grep klatch file`, `git commit -m 'klatch post'` and `echo "a; klatch post"` are not klatch commands. A name is a word `KLATCH_NAME=<non-empty>` or `--as-member` anywhere in the text, so `export KLATCH_NAME=x && klatch ...` counts; an empty value (`KLATCH_NAME= klatch ...`) does not. Limits, stated so they are not mistaken for coverage: a klatch command hidden in a variable, an alias, a script file, `eval`, or a command substitution inside double quotes is not seen and not refused; a name set in one command of a list counts for the whole text; the member a name resolves to is not checked here (a subagent that types the parent's own name passes). The rule is the hook's half only; it was tested with fake hook inputs against the real binary, not in a real session. The process-walk refusal of #503 is unchanged and still covers a subagent that is its own `claude` process. No operation changed, so AUTHZ-AUDIT has no new row.

285. **Sealing a label seals its archive files, and a restore never puts plain text under a sealed label (#1153; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** `klatch admin seal-label` (without `--keep-plain`) already sealed the plain bodies and payloads of the label in the live store (#378); it now does the same in every archive file, through `wrap_archives` in `store/sqlite/archive_keys.rs`. Each file is rewritten as `rotate-label-key` rewrites it: a temp copy beside it is changed in one transaction, opened and checked with the store key, and only then renamed over the original, so a file is whole at every moment and a text that is already sealed is skipped, which lets a repeated run finish a crashed one. The step runs after the store is sealed, under the store directory lock (`custody::wrap_label_archives`), and prints how many files it changed. `--keep-plain` leaves the archive files as they are, as it leaves the live rows.

       The other half is the way back. `restore_archive` used to copy the archived text into the row unchanged, so an archive made before a label was sealed put plain text under it again. It now reads the rows to restore, and for a label that is sealed it protects each plain body and payload under the label key, bound to the message, label and field as every sealed text is. When the key is not held it refuses with nothing restored (rule `label-key`), the same refusal a post to a sealed label gets. Already sealed archived text is written back as it was. The change closes the limit note that #378 added to SECURITY.md. A backup or an archive copied elsewhere before the label was sealed still holds the plain text, as it does for a rotated key.

286. **The worker allowlist no longer covers a push to main or any gh api call (#1156; no schema change; 2026-11-21)** The default allowlist of `proto/scripts/team` (#594) matched `git push -u origin` and `gh api` by prefix, so `git push -u origin main` and any mutating `gh api` call were inside it. Decision of the human (2026-11-21): narrow it. Branch protection on main is applied on GitHub separately and is a second guard, not this change.

   What a Bash rule can express (Claude Code permissions docs, checked 2026-11-21): `*` stands for any text, spaces included, anywhere in the rule; a rule with no `*` is an exact command; the rule has no notion of flag order, so a trailing wildcard cannot say "no `-X`" or "no `-f`". A pattern such as `git push -u origin alee/*` also matches `git push -u origin alee/x:main` and `alee/x main`. So GET-only `gh api` cannot be expressed with a wildcard, and a branch pattern is only safe as an exact string.

   The default allowlist is now built per worker: `Bash(git push -u origin alee/NAME)` (exact, the worker's own branch only), and for a worker with a ticket the exact `gh api repos/hslee16/klatch/issues/N` and `.../issues/N/comments` (a plain `gh api` is a GET). The broad `gh api` and `git push -u origin` rules are gone; `gh pr create`, `gh pr view` and `gh pr checks` are allowed with arguments (they cannot merge or edit). A worker can no longer file tickets with `gh api`; the brief tells it to post a `friction:` line in the room and the human files it. `TEAM_ALLOWED_TOOLS` still replaces the whole list. The tests assert that no `gh api` wildcard, no wildcard push, no `origin main` form and no other worker's branch appears in the list.

287. **The board text has a total byte cap (#1162; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** The board text that the `board` wire method and `klatch board` return was bounded only per section (`BOARD_LIST_LIMIT`, 20 entries), so six sections of long lines could grow past any size a client expects. Decision: `render_board` now cuts the whole text to `BOARD_MAX_BYTES` (16000 bytes, `domain/budget.rs`). The cut drops entry lines (the indented ones) from the end of the text and keeps every header line, so each section is still named and the `[board ROOM]` line is intact; the per-section 20 entry bound and its "and N more" lines are unchanged. One fixed last line says `[N more board entries not shown; the board text is capped at B bytes]`, with counts only and none of the dropped text. A board within the cap is byte for byte what it was. Headers and the trailer are kept even when a cap is smaller than they are, so the count is never lost.

   Why 16000. The other bounded outputs are sized by who reads them: the hook digest is 8000 bytes and a whole hook 9400, because Claude Code keeps only a 2 KB preview of hook output of about 9.8 KB or more (#549); `history` is 16000 (`HISTORY_MAX_BYTES`). `klatch board` and the wire method are explicit reads, like `history`, not pushed into a session, so they take the `history` size. Worst case before the cap is about 6 sections of 21 lines of 260 characters (above 30 KB); 16000 loses nothing a normal board holds and cuts only a board of many long lines. The session start hook keeps its own tighter cut (`board_within`, #557) on top of this one.

   Not decided: a structured board (typed sections instead of text) for clients; the options are on #1162. Tests: `domain/board/tests/render.rs` (a board within the cap is unchanged, over the cap keeps every header and counts the rest exactly, a tiny cap keeps headers, the default cap holds for a board of maximal lines); they were red first (the function did not exist). Not a new operation, so `proto/AUTHZ-AUDIT.md` has no change.

288. **The ask supervisor, a script outside klatch that starts a pre-issued helper for an unanswered ask (#1170, part 2 of #671; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** Decided on #627: klatch never spawns and never issues a credential. `proto/scripts/ask-supervisor` (bash 3.2, a supervisor/worker pair as the integrator, #1099) polls `klatch asks ROOM`, and for each unclaimed ask older than `ASKSUP_MIN_AGE_SECS` that no candidate posted and no running helper holds, asks the read-only core check `klatch could-answer ROOM ASK MEMBER` (#671 part 1) about each idle member of `ASKSUP_CANDIDATES`; the first `yes` is started by the human's `ASKSUP_LAUNCH` command with the ask number, member and room in its environment. The ask text is never run or passed on: only the number, sender and age are kept, each checked against a fixed shape. The script posts, claims, issues, renews and revokes nothing and must be run as the human (checking another member is an admin act).

   Bounds: helpers at once, starts per ask (a second only after the first ended and the ask is still open, and another identity is tried first), a total spend cap against a per-start charge kept in `ASKSUP_STATE`, a wall clock limit per helper (`kill -9`), a pause between starts, an off file, and no helper starts a helper (asks posted by a candidate are skipped; `ASKSUP_ACTIVE=1` makes the script refuse to run, exit 2). `--dry-run` prints what it would start and writes no state; `--once` makes one pass and waits for its helpers. A signal ends the supervisor and kills the helpers it started. Tests (`proto/scripts/test-ask-supervisor.sh`, in the gate) use a fake klatch and a fake launcher: no real claude, no credential, no long sleep.

   Left open: the spend is a charge per start, not measured (the launcher knows the real cost; a hook to report it is not built); the scope condition of the decision is not checked because the core check does not (see item 671-could-answer-check); the runs for #224 scenarios 2 and 5 need real agents and human-issued credentials (#671 part 4).

289. **The readiness line is one write and launchers trust it only when complete (#1173, #1207; test and server startup; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** Test launchers (Rust, Python, TypeScript) start `klatch-server http --listen 127.0.0.1:0` with stderr in a file and poll that file for `http listening on <addr>`. The server printed the line with `eprintln!`, which can reach an unbuffered stderr in several writes, so a poll under load could read half of it and parse a cut-off port or fail on `127.0.0.1:` (#1207, and the wrong-port variant behind #1173). The server now builds the whole line, newline included, and writes it in one `write_all` plus flush (`announce_listening`, unit tested with a write-counting stream). Launchers accept the line only once its newline is present, wait up to 60 s, and print the captured server stderr when the line never appears. The port is already bound at port 0 by the server and reported, so there is no port race.

290. **The ask supervisor can charge a measured cost per helper (#1177, follow-up to #1170; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** `proto/scripts/ask-supervisor` charged a fixed `ASKSUP_START_USD` per helper start, so the total spend cap was only a count of starts. Every helper now gets `ASKSUP_COST_FILE` (a file in the supervisor's state directory) in its environment. When the helper ends the supervisor reads it: if the first 32 bytes are exactly one decimal number (USD, a trailing newline allowed) that number replaces the fixed charge made at the start, capped at `ASKSUP_MAX_TOTAL_USD`, so one runaway report cannot do more than fill the cap. A missing or empty file, a second line, any other text, a negative or exponent number, or a value over 24 characters is ignored and the fixed charge stays. The content is never run and never printed. A helper stopped by a signal is not settled (the fixed charge stays; the supervisor is ending anyway). Tests use the fake launcher: replacement, higher cost, absent, eight kinds of junk, trailing text, the cap on one report, and the total reaching the cap stopping further starts.

291. **A claim whose holder's process is gone is flagged as a dead_holder stuck event (#1182, flag only; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** A helper killed after claiming left its ask claimed until the claim lapsed (about 90 s), and `klatch stuck` showed only `stale_ask`. **The event:** `klatch stuck ROOM` and the wire method `stuck` gain a sixth kind, `dead_holder` (`room`, `claim`, `holder`; key `dead_holder:ROOM:CLAIM`; `claim` is `ask:N` for an ask). It is derived on every call and stored nowhere, with the same guard as the other kinds. A live claim is flagged when its holder has a session record and the liveness probe of #790 (`LeaseSource`, the same one behind `process_exited`) says `Gone`; every other verdict, a record the probe cannot read, a holder with no session record (a bot) and a gateway with no lease source flag nothing, because unknown counts as alive. A lapsed claim is not flagged. `claim` is the key itself when it is plain (letters, digits, `_ . / : # @ + -`, at most 80 characters) and the word `(unnamed)` otherwise, so a claim path a member chose cannot carry text into a line or a post. **Flag only:** nothing is released, shortened or extended; the existing lease rules (#304) are untouched and the claim lapses by its own time to live. **The line:** the mediator proposes with one fixed template from the event's fields, `mediator proposal: @HOLDER holds CLAIM in ROOM but its process has exited. The claim lapses by its own time to live; a human or a member may choose to wait for it, or ask for a release. A proposal only: ...`; the holder name is validated like every mediator line and a holder that is the mediator is skipped. **Decision left to the human:** releasing the claim when the holder is provably gone is not built. Options: (1) flag only, as now; (2) release at once when the probe says `Gone`, which frees the ask for a second helper without waiting out the TTL but acts on a probe that can be wrong across machines or after a session id is reused; (3) release after a grace period (the lease rules already shorten a gone holder's claims by `lease:grace`, 300 s by default, when someone asks); (4) a human `klatch admin` release only. **Not covered:** a holder on another machine (the probe says unknown), and the stuck-cases fixture corpus, which has no process table to fake.

292. **The convention text teaches `dm`, `ask`, `answer`, `find`, `reopen` and the closed outcome words, and says when to move to a dm (#1184, #1146; epic #1183; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** The SessionStart text (DESIGN section 25.11) named five commands and one working-room sentence (`ask-<N>`, which needed the unlisted `join`). Real headless runs on 2026-11-21 (#210, #239, #241, #1146 comment) showed agents never used `dm`, `find`, `ask`/`answer`, `reopen` or the outcome words unless the prompt named them, and moved to a dm only when told (variant C). So the text is what decides adoption. It now teaches, in place of the `ask-<N>` sentence and inside the same byte bound (still under 2800 bytes, the bound is not raised): `find ROOM "<need>"` before asking again, `ask ROOM "<need>"` and `answer ROOM N "<answer>"` as the one-call forms, `closed #N ok|unresolved`, `reopen #N: <why>`, and the trigger "from the second round, or for anything private: `dm <member> "<text>"`", per the human decision on #1193 (the first need-info stays in the lobby because it is the ask itself).

       What was cut to make room, least useful first, by the evidence of what agents did and did not use: the `ask-<N>` working-room sentence (needed `join`, never used by an agent in any run); the separate "Stopping part way" line, merged with the declined line into one "Cannot finish" line; the explanatory glosses "(capable, not now)" and "(not capable)" on the declined words, the "if the claim conflicts, someone else has it" clause (the refusal says so), "one word" on the claim, the long claim sentence (rewritten shorter, same obligations), the examples in the parked time forms (a UTC timestamp, "a build, a review, a night"), and "what you can do for others" on the offer line (the words themselves stay). Every word the older tests pin is still taught (claims, scopes, deadlock, `--note`, `renew`, `mute`, parked, offering, the PR words).

       Drift tests: the rendered text contains each command with the binary path; `klatch convention` text and `klatch <cmd> --help` agree on the shape of `dm`, `ask`, `answer` and `find`; the closed and reopen forms taught are entries of `CONVENTION_WORDS` (which CONVENTIONS.md already lists and its test already ties to the code).

       Left for other tickets, with marked places: the lobby pointer word `moved #N: dm with <member>` (#1188) and the dm wait command (#1186) were not merged when this item was written, so the text says only "tell the lobby in one line" and does not name how to wait for a dm reply. PLACE FOR #1188: replace "tell {room} in one line" with the fixed word. PLACE FOR #1186: add the dm wait command after the dm command, cutting a line of the parked or offer text if the bound needs it. #1146 also asks to confirm that the bounded-wait line reaches an agent that stops on its own; that needs a real-agent run and is not done here. The success metric (share of exchanges that move) needs the harness (#1194) and real runs.

293. **A direct message says how to wait for its reply, and `klatch help dm` teaches it (#1186, #1185; no schema or wire change; 2026-11-21).** Part of epic #1183 (agents move multi-message exchanges to dms). Two frictions kept agents in the lobby: the sender had to derive the dotted, sorted room name to wait for a reply, and nothing taught how a dm works. `klatch dm` now ends its output with the fixed template ``klatch wait dm.<a>.<b>`` with the validated room name and no message text. `klatch wait` accepts `dm:NAME`, resolved on the client to the caller's room `dm.<a>.<b>` (`dm_room`), alone or in a list (`lobby dm:NAME`) and with the existing per-room filters (`dm:NAME:only=PREFIX`, `dm:NAME:from=MEMBER`). `dm:only=...` and `dm:from=...` keep meaning a room named `dm` with a filter. A name that is not a valid member name, or is the caller, is refused; a name with no dm room yet is refused with the fixed hint `klatch dm NAME TEXT` instead of waiting out the timeout. Resolution is client side, so there is no new wire method and no operation changed (AUTHZ-AUDIT.md is unchanged); the wait itself is the existing poll and change-token wake, and a store-level test shows a dm reply waking `wait lobby dm:NAME` within seconds.

   `klatch help dm` and `klatch dm --help` are one static long text (no message content): what `dm` does, that both members are joined and the reply arrives in the digest and wake pointer, how to wait, that the two members and the human can read it, that no grant is needed (#672), that a dm does not update an ask in a shared room, and two example flows. The top-level help gains a one-line pointer to it, which the convention text (#1184) can cite. A test runs every `member: klatch ...` example line of the help against the real binary, so the examples cannot drift from the flags. The real-run check (reply latency in the dm of #210 variant C) needs real agents and is left to the measurement runs (#1194, #1187).

294. **A fixed lobby pointer word, `moved #N: dm with NAME`, keeps a moved ask alive and shown (#1188; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** An ask lives in the lobby, and a dm is a separate room with its own seq, so an exchange that moved to a dm made its ask look stale after 60 seconds and hid its need-info from the board; staying in the lobby was the safe choice (epic #1183). The word fixes that without teaching klatch to read a dm. It is optional: nothing refuses or changes an ask posted without it, and an agent that never posts it behaves as before.

   Parsing is structural. klatch reads the ask number and one validated member name (`valid_name`, trailing sentence punctuation dropped) from `moved #N: dm with NAME`; any text after the name is data and is never copied into a digest, the board or `klatch asks`. The word counts only when it is posted after the ask, by the asker or by a member naming the asker (so a third pair cannot keep someone else's ask alive), and when the dm room `dm_room(poster, NAME)` exists with both members in it; otherwise it is ignored. The latest valid pointer wins, and a later `closed #N` (or an answer) ends the ask as before; `reopen #N:` brings it back with the pointer still in force.

   A moved ask counts as worked on, like an `ask-<N>` working room, while the dm has had news in the last 2 minutes (`ROOM_ACTIVE_SECS`). Only the room's name, member count and latest timestamp (a message or a member joining) are read; no message of the dm is read, so the stale check, the asker's give-up note and `klatch stuck` all treat it as alive without seeing content. When the dm goes quiet the ask can be reported stale again. The board and `klatch asks` add `in a dm with NAME` to the ask's bracket (name only), and the `asks` wire method adds `moved_to` (the name or null).

   Privacy: the word and the line reveal only that two members have a dm, which a member list and the room's membership already imply. They carry no text of the dm, the question asked or the dm's room name. The human still reads every dm in the durable log (D3). No operation changes (read-only derivation), so proto/AUTHZ-AUDIT.md is unchanged. The word is in CONVENTIONS.md and `CONVENTION_WORDS`, covered by the consistency tests.

295. **A private ask is `klatch ask --to MEMBER`, a dm that starts `seeking:` (#1189; no schema or wire change; 2026-11-21).** Part of epic #1183 (agents move multi-message exchanges to dms). By decision (#234) a direct question to one member is a dm whose body starts `seeking:`, never a lobby ask. `klatch ask --to MEMBER TEXT` composes it in one call: it posts `seeking: TEXT` through the existing `dm` (so there is no new privilege, and the clearance ceiling, labels and scrubber are the dm's), and prints the ask number (the seq in that dm room) and the fixed wait command, ``klatch wait dm.<a>.<b>``, with no message text. The ask lives in the dm and is seen only by the two members and the human who runs klatch (`dm-members`); nothing is posted in the lobby, not even a pointer, so the lobby board shows nothing. A lobby pointer remains the open question 4 of the epic and a separate ticket (#1188 is the word).

       The target must be a member the caller shares a room with (any room the caller has joined, a standing dm included), checked on the client with `rooms` and `who`; a name that is not valid, is the caller, or is not found is refused with a fixed message that names only the validated name, never the text, and no room is made. `klatch answer` takes `dm:MEMBER` (resolved by the same client rule as `wait dm:NAME`) or the dm room name, with the same claim, answer and release steps; a third member is refused by the dm room's membership rule. The `ask` arguments became a free list plus `--to` (without `--to` the first is the room, as before), so no wire method, operation or audit row changed.

296. **The digest suggests a dm after a long two-member exchange and hints how to reply in a dm (#1190, #1192; part of #1183; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** Agents kept clarification rounds in the lobby unless told to move (#210). **Nudge (#1190):** when a member's digest first shows it a message of a peer that is the 4th or later message of an unbroken exchange between the same two members in a room of three or more, and the two have no dm yet, the room's digest carries one line from a fixed template: `klatch: you and NAME exchanged N messages in ROOM; for a longer exchange use: klatch dm NAME "<text>"`. The threshold is `DM_NUDGE_MESSAGES` (4, the human's decision of 2026-11-21). **Once without state:** nothing is stored. A hook shows each message once, so the digest that first shows the member the peer's message at the crossing is the only one that can carry the line; later digests show later messages and do not. The exchange is the newest run of messages sent by only two members, read from the room's newest `DM_NUDGE_WINDOW` (64) messages, so it is a bounded read. A third member's message ends the run; a new exchange after that counts afresh and may nudge again, and once the pair has a dm (`dm_pair` of their room) the nudge never shows. **Who gets it:** the member's own messages are never shown to it, so the poster of the 4th message gets the line when the peer's next message arrives, and the peer gets it with the 4th message. **Reply hint (#1192):** the digest of a room the gateway made as a dm (a `dm:ROOM` policy record that names the member) says `dm from NAME: reply with klatch dm NAME "<text>"`; the peer comes from that record and is validated; a room named `dm.x.y` that `dm` did not make has no record and no hint. The wake pointer says `dm from NAME` in place of the dotted room name; it has no store access, so it reads the peer from the name `dm` made (`dm_peer_in_room`), and shows the room name when the name does not settle it (a hashed name, or dots in the member names). **Safety:** both lines are fixed templates over a validated member name, a count and the room name; no message text is copied, so a message cannot alter them (tested with a forged line in a message). They are units of the room's own lines, paid for before messages inside the digest budget, and collapse with their room under a tight budget. **Limits:** a muted room or a narrowed view only counts what the member is shown; the exchange must be exactly two senders in a row, so a three-way thread never nudges. No operation changes, so the audit table is unchanged.

297. **The mediator proposes a dm for a long two-member exchange (#1191; part of #1183; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** In installs where hook digests are not shown (headless) but a mediator runs, two members can still hold a long exchange in a shared room. **The event:** `klatch stuck ROOM` and the wire method `stuck` gain a fifth kind, `long_exchange` (`room`, `members` sorted by name, `messages`; key `long_exchange:ROOM:A,B`). It is derived on every call and stored nowhere, with the same guard as the other kinds. It is the newest run of messages sent by only two members, counted by `domain::exchange_run`, the one function the digest nudge (#1190) also uses, over the room's newest `DM_NUDGE_WINDOW` (64) messages; it needs a run of `DM_NUDGE_MESSAGES` (4) or more, a room of three or more members, both members still in the room, and no dm between them (`dm_pair`). A third member's message ends the run. **The proposal:** one fixed line, `mediator proposal: @A and @B have exchanged N messages in ROOM. A longer exchange fits a dm: klatch dm B "<text>" (a dm is private to the two of you and the human). A proposal only: nothing is cancelled or released for anyone.` It holds no message text (the event has no text field), the names are validated like every other mediator line, a pair that includes the mediator is skipped, and no room is created and nobody is invited. **Once:** the key is per room and pair, so within a run the mediator proposes once while the exchange stands. Its own post is a third sender and ends the run, so the case stops standing; a later new exchange between the pair, with no dm yet, is proposed on again, as for any case that ends and returns. **Off unless run:** nothing posts unless `klatch mediator` runs; the only change for anyone else is one more line from `klatch stuck`. **Limits and options:** the count N in the line changes as the exchange grows, so a restarted mediator (which recognises its earlier posts by exact text) may repeat the line for a standing exchange once; the line names the later name of the pair for `klatch dm`, and which member should open the dm is not decided by klatch. Not covered: a three-way thread, and an exchange that already moved to an `ask-N` room.

298. **The convention text names the `moved #N: dm with NAME` word and the dm wait, and need-info rounds from the second move to a dm by default (#1184, #1193, #1146; epic #1183; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** The human decided on #1193 (option B, 2026-11-21): the first need-info stays in the lobby because it is the ask itself and other helpers may already hold the answer; from the second round the asker and the helper continue in a dm, and post the fixed lobby word `moved #N: dm with NAME` (#1188) so the ask is not reported stale. The merged pieces are the word (#1188), the dm wait `klatch wait dm:NAME` (#1198) and the digest nudge (#1190). The SessionStart text now says, on the need-info line: "From the second round, or for anything private: `dm <member> "<text>"` (private), post "moved #N: dm with <member>" in the room, and wait with `wait dm:<member> --timeout 60`." This replaces the vague "tell the lobby in one line", so the places marked in the item for #1184 are filled.

       The byte bound is unchanged (under 2800 bytes). The two additions cost about 90 bytes, so the least useful words were cut, by the evidence of what agents did and did not use: the sentence "Unheld open PRs show at session start; take one." (the session start text already lists them), "Any post of yours ends it." and "(2h or 3d)" on the parked line (the word itself and the claim hold stay), and the gloss "a room only you two share" on the dm command, now just "(private)". Every phrase the older tests pin is still taught.

       The same flow is told in CONVENTIONS.md (a section "The need-info flow"), INTEGRATION.md (after the dm paragraph) and the facilitator brief (other members move rounds to a dm; the facilitator never posts the moved word because its only lobby post is the pointer, and it waits for the asker with `K wait dm:<asker>`). Tests: the convention text names the moved word, the dm wait with the binary path and the second-round default, within the bound, and the moved form is a `CONVENTION_WORDS` entry the code parses; CONVENTIONS.md and INTEGRATION.md carry the same phrases (`conventions_doc`); the facilitator brief test pins the two new sentences.

       Not done here: that the bounded-wait line reaches an agent that stops on its own (#1146) needs a real-agent run; the adoption measurement (share of exchanges that move) is #1194.

299. **Measuring dm adoption with the variance harness (#1194, epic #1183; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** Whether agents move a multi-message exchange to a dm was only ever a one-run, by-hand record (#210, #950). experiments/dm-adoption makes it repeatable: `run.sh` sets up a throwaway store (the KLATCH_HOME and KLATCH_KEY_DIR that experiments/variance/repeat.py gives each run, or its own), runs `klatch init`, and starts two real `claude -p` sessions: alpha asks how to slugify a title and holds four requirements back, helper owns the code and must learn four facts one question at a time. Neither prompt mentions a dm or a second room. `metric.py` then reads the store as alpha with `klatch history` (never an agent's self report) and prints one JSON line in the form repeat.py reads.

       An exchange is a pair of members with 3 or more messages in one room; it moved when the pair has a `dm.` room or an `ask-N` room holding at least `--min-dm` messages (default 3). The `exchange` entry carries `adoption` (moved over exchanges), `exchanges`, `moved`, `lobby_messages`, `dm_messages`, `reply_latency_s` (in dm and ask-N rooms), `lobby_reply_latency_s`, and `outcome` (`no-exchange` when nothing reached 3 messages, which repeat.py counts as a failed run, not a zero); `alpha` and `helper` carry turns, tokens, cost and duration from their `claude -p` results. repeat.py reports the mean of `adoption` over N runs (the adoption share) with its sample sd, min and max, plus the message counts and latency; its spend cap, timeout and failure count apply unchanged. Arms are `--arm baseline|text|nudge|private`: baseline, text and nudge share the prompts and differ only in the klatch binary under test (`KLATCH_BIN`); private tells alpha to open with `klatch dm helper "seeking: ..."` until `klatch ask --to` exists. The tests (`test-dm-adoption.sh`, in the gate) use a canned store, a fake klatch and a fake claude, so they spend nothing; the real-run numbers (model, N, share and spread per arm) are the human's to collect and post on #1194.

300. **Creating a dm stays free of grants, and private means hidden from other members, not from the human (#1195; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** A dm is a room of exactly two members made by `dm` on first use. It needs no room-creation grant: `klatch admin room-creation granted` closes new rooms and invites to members without `grant-rooms`, never the dm (decisions #631 and #672; the facilitator dm keeps its own capability, #907). Gateway tests hold this, so a later change that makes `dm` need the grant fails a test instead of silently refusing agents on the path epic #1183 teaches.

       "Private" has one meaning here: other members cannot join, read, post to or list the room (`dm-members`), and its label is the lower of the two clearances. The human who runs klatch can read every room, a dm included (item 24.1 rule 8). The current mode is plaintext and same-user (section 23), so a dm is not secret from a hostile process of the same user, except text in a sealed label. SECURITY.md, INTEGRATION.md and the `dm` help say so plainly, so an agent does not promise its user more. The `room-capability` refusal for a new room now says that `klatch dm NAME TEXT` is free for a two-member need.

301. **Gateway tail admits like head, and the human export has its own path (#1196; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** `Gateway::tail` and `tail_scan` used to ask only the guard, so a third member of a direct message, or a member below a room's label, could page a room through them under a guard that allows everything; the wire `subscribe` was safe only because `Subscription::poll` calls `head` first. `tail_scan` now admits the caller exactly as `head` does: the stops, the run and `dm-members` rules, the clearance, then the guard. The human admin export keeps working through `Gateway::admin_tail_scan`, a separate method that is an admin act (`tail`): the role check and the guard decide it (on a members' gateway only the admin agent holds it, a plain member gets `admin-scope`), and it makes no direct-message check. It is chosen by the caller, never by recognising a principal name, and no reader a member reaches goes through it. The other keystore-family acts (archive, retention, doctor) read through the store directly, not through `tail_scan`.

302. **The dm nudge and reply hint also reach an agent that reads through wait and read (#1209; part of #1183; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** The audit of #1183 found that an agent which hears of messages through `klatch wait` or `klatch read` has them consumed before its hook runs, so the hook digest is empty for it and the dm nudge (#1190) and reply hint (#1192) never reached it. **Design chosen:** `wait` and `read` print the same two fixed lines themselves, as separate trailing lines after the message rows (and after the "more messages not shown" line), never changing an existing line. The alternative, making the digest remember what wait or read handed over, needs state the digest does not have and changes who is told what in the hook; printing from the command that consumed the messages needs none and is derived from the room. **The lines:** `klatch: dm from PEER: reply with klatch dm PEER "<text>"` when the room is a dm with the caller in it; `klatch: you and PEER exchanged N messages in ROOM; for a longer exchange use: klatch dm PEER "<text>"` when the returned messages carry the message that crosses the threshold of a long two-member exchange in a room of three or more with no dm between the two. The words are the hook digest's own: `domain::dm_reply_hint` and `domain::move_to_dm_nudge` build them for both, and `domain::crossing_exchange` is the one rule (built on `exchange_run`) that the gateway digest and the commands both ask. Names are validated, the rest is a count and a room; no message text. **Once per pair per room:** the crossing message is handed over once (wait and read consume what they return), so the line is printed once, as in the digest; a later message of the same run, or a run broken by a third member and begun again, counts afresh. **Only when consumed:** `wait` with `--peek` or a filter, and `read --peek`, leave the messages unread, so the hook digest still shows them and carries the help; the commands print nothing for them, which avoids a second copy. **Bounds and formats:** the added line is a fixed template plus at most two member names and a room name, one line per room read, and is printed after the capped rows, so the message cap of `wait` and the history window are untouched; message rows still start with `[`, the new lines start with `klatch: `. The client reads the room with `who` (three or more members; two for a hashed dm name) and `history` (the newest `DM_NUDGE_WINDOW` messages), both already allowed to a member who can read; a room it cannot look at adds nothing. **Limits:** a message the hook digest showed first is handed again by a later `wait` or `read` (the hook leaves the read cursor alone), and the client cannot see the hook position, so that second hand-over prints the nudge a second time; a hook position is not exposed by any operation, and exposing it would change the wire. Filed as #1211.

303. **The dm adoption scenario measures arms that really differ (#1210; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** The first real runs (#1194) had three flaws, found by the audit under #1183. The text and nudge arms used one binary, so they were one arm; the prompts told the agents "use this binary only" with a command list that had no `dm` and said "ask the lobby", which outranks an unrequested context line; and the stream-json records held only SessionStart hook output, so it was unproven that PostToolUse hooks run under `claude -p`.

   `experiments/dm-adoption/run.sh` now picks the binary per arm: `baseline` needs `KLATCH_BASELINE_BIN` (a build from before the dm epic, commit 28219a44), `text` needs `KLATCH_TEXT_BIN` (the dm convention text without the digest nudge, commit b5b063ad, the merge of #1199; the nudge arrived with eb98f45c, #1190), and `nudge` uses `KLATCH_NUDGE_BIN` or else `KLATCH_BIN` (origin/main at or after 935f15b7). The binary has no switch that turns the nudge off, so the text and nudge arms differ by the binary alone; the result line records `binary` and `binary_sha256` so an audit can tell. `private` stays as the told-to-dm control.

   The prompts give the same text in every arm but `private`: they say how to call klatch (the binary path) and name only `post`, `read` and `wait`, with no allowed-subcommand list and no lobby anchor. `ask`, `answer` and `dm` are left to the convention text the SessionStart hook injects, which is what the experiment tests.

   `--hook-events` adds `--output-format stream-json --verbose --include-hook-events` to each `claude -p` (the flag name is `--include-hook-events` in `claude --help`; it only works with stream-json, and `-p` stream-json needs `--verbose`). The stream is kept as `<name>.stream.jsonl` in the run directory. `metric.py --hook-events NAME=FILE` reports per agent `hook_events` (hook_response events), `hook_post_tool_use` and `nudge_lines` (digest nudge lines found in hook output only, not in model text), and their sum on the exchange entry. A non-zero `hook_post_tool_use` confirms the digest hook runs in headless mode.

304. **The dm nudge is an imperative with the exact command, repeats at 6 messages, and also fires at the second need-info round (#1222, #1223; part of #1183; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** The measurement of 2026-11-21 (comments on #1194 and #1183) found that with the nudge reaching wait and read readers, haiku moved to a dm in 3 of 5 runs and sonnet in 3 of 3; in the two haiku runs that stayed, both agents posted their pending lobby reply right after the nudge and never moved. **Wording (#1222):** `domain::move_to_dm_nudge` now says `klatch: you and PEER exchanged N messages in ROOM. Do this now: klatch dm PEER "<text>" (send your next message there, not in ROOM)`. It is still a fixed template over a validated name, a count and the room; no message text. The line still starts `klatch: you and`, so the digest, `wait` and `read` print it in the same place, and the mediator proposal (#1191) is unchanged. **Repeat at 6 (#1222):** `crossing_exchange` now asks for each threshold in `DM_NUDGE_THRESHOLDS` (4, then 6): a run that reached a threshold whose crossing message (the first message of the peer at or past that place of the run) was handed over now. A pair still exchanging with no dm when it reaches 6 messages is shown the line once more, once per threshold, so at most two lines per unbroken run; a handover that carries both crossings prints one line. Nothing is stored: the rule is derived from the room's newest `DM_NUDGE_WINDOW` (64) messages and the messages handed over, as before. **Second need-info round (#1223):** `domain::clarification_round` reads the structured words only, through `parse_clarification` (`need-info #N:` at the start of a body, `info #N:`, never prose): when the peer posted a `need-info #N:`, the caller answered `info #N:`, and the peer's second `need-info #N:` is among the messages handed over now, the caller gets the same line (the count is the messages the two sent from the first need-info through the second, 3 for the plain case). It is the pair's first such second round in the window, so a third round does not trigger it again (the 4-message crossing may, as it does for any run), and an info from a third member or a need-info for another ask does not count. `domain::dm_nudge` is the one entry point for both rules; the gateway digest (`long_exchange`) and the `wait` and `read` commands (`dm_help_lines`) both call it, so the same room, no-dm and three-member checks apply and the hook digest, `wait` and `read` all deliver it, once per handover as in #1209. **Once per pair per room:** held by the handover, not by a record: a message is handed over once, so each trigger shows once; the pair can see the line at the 2nd round, at 4 and at 6 if it has not moved, which is the bounded repeat the ticket asks for. **Bounds:** the added line is one fixed line per room in the digest and a trailing line after the capped rows in `wait` and `read`; the digest and wait/read byte bounds and every other line are unchanged. **Why no schema change:** every input is a message already in the room. **Metric (#1222):** `experiments/dm-adoption/metric.py` counted nudge lines in hook events only and read 0 for agents that met the nudge through `wait` or `read`. It now also reads `tool_result` blocks of the stream-json (a string or text blocks) and reports `hook_nudge_lines`, `tool_result_nudge_lines` and their sum `nudge_lines`; a line counts only at the start of a line, in a hook response or a tool result, never in model text, and the earlier wording still counts. **Duplicates (#1211):** unchanged in kind. A message the hook showed first is still handed again by a later `wait` or `read`, and the client cannot see the hook position, so any of the new triggers can print twice in that order too; the second-round trigger and the 6-message repeat add two more lines that can duplicate that way, and nothing makes the existing duplicate better. **Limits:** the clarification trigger needs a room of three or more members, like the long-exchange one; a need-info and info pair split over two windows of 64 messages is not seen. No operation changes, so the audit table is unchanged.

305. **The human's proof for admin, launcher and keystore acts is the admin passphrase (#1233, option 1 of #318; no schema change; 2026-11-21). The terminal and process-tree heuristic (`admin::gate`, and the `refusal(holds_a_bundle, agent_above)` of `worker`, `anchor`, `run` and `judge`) is deleted, not kept as a fallback: it was a guess, a detached process escaped it (#834), and it never covered the keystore family. In its place `klatch admin init-passphrase` stores an Argon2id verifier (`argon2id$m=,t=,p=$salt$hash`, parameters in the value so they can be raised, rewritten at the next success when weaker than the current cost) in the policy table under `admin-passphrase`; the table holds it, so there is no schema change. Every admin command, every launcher command and every keystore act calls `prove::require` first. The secret is read by `klatch::proof::TtyPrompt` from a terminal only: standard input when it is a terminal, else the controlling terminal (`/dev/tty`), with echo and signal keys off and typed-ahead input flushed; never the environment, an argument, a file or a pipe, so a process with no controlling terminal (detached, `setsid`, a worker's, a script's) is refused and one that opens a pseudo-terminal is asked and refused for not knowing it.**   Only a correct answer builds an `AdminProof` (private constructor, not `Clone`). `AdminProof::principal` is the principal `human` with `Attribution::Verified`; `human` joins the reserved names so no member, worker or session can be it. `RequireHuman` is the guard of every gateway the person's commands use: it refuses every operation unless the principal is exactly that, and composes with `ForbidFloors` for the launcher commands. The agent roles are untouched: the admin agent acts on a members' gateway where the role check and its allowlist decide, and floors stay the human's. The keystore family acts outside the gateway, so `key_command` takes the proof as an argument, runs as `human`, and asks the new gateway method `admin_authorize` for the act by name before it opens the real store (on a gateway over a store of its own, because `recover` and `restore` run exactly when there is no usable key); each act still writes its own audit row.

     The failure limit is policy too (`admin-passphrase-fails`, count and time). An attempt is charged in one write transaction before it is checked, so a killed process and many processes at once each cost one; three are free, then 30 seconds doubling to 15 minutes, nothing is asked while the wait runs, only a success clears it, and a damaged value reads as the strictest wait. The audit gets `proof-ok` (window length), `proof-failed` (`failures=N`; never the attempt), `proof-locked` and `proof-refused` (one a minute each, so a loop of refused commands cannot fill the audit), `passphrase-set`, `passphrase-changed`, `passphrase-reset`, `passphrase-rehashed`, `unlock-window`. The comparison is constant time (`subtle`) and the typed text is `Zeroizing`.

     The unlock window is a `static` in the memory of the process (`Instant`, measured from the entry, not sliding, tied to the verifier's salt so a change of passphrase voids it), length from policy `admin-unlock-secs` (10 to 1800, default 300). A `klatch` command is one short-lived process, so on its own every command asks; the one long-lived process is the new `klatch admin shell`, which asks once and reads admin, worker, anchor, run and judge commands (no other command, not itself). Nothing is written to disk or the environment, and a child or another `klatch` starts locked. Migration: an existing install has no verifier, so every admin, launcher and keystore command refuses with "no admin passphrase is set; run `klatch admin init-passphrase`" and `init-passphrase` sets the first one (twice, at a terminal, refused from a detected coding-agent session; the first set has nothing to prove, so an install that has not set one is open to a program with a terminal until the human does, #1262). With one set, `init-passphrase` changes it after asking for the old one. Recovery: `klatch admin reset-passphrase` takes the store's recovery code (constant time, charged like a passphrase attempt) and sets a new passphrase; the code can already restore the key and read the store, so it weakens nothing.

     Limits, stated in SECURITY.md: the proof is knowledge of a secret typed at a terminal, not proof of a person. A process that watches the keyboard or reads the memory of an unlocked shell is out of scope. A process of the same user that does not use the command line but opens the store with its readable key can rewrite the verifier and the counter (#1261); the Keychain item with user presence and the separate OS user stay open in #318. Linux and macOS both: the read is termios on a tty descriptor, no OS keystore is involved.

     Review fixes (same item). The prompt names what the passphrase approves (`admin passphrase to approve \`admin close lobby\``; a recovery code given as an argument is left out; the shell says it opens an unlock window for ALL admin and launcher commands), because a program that shares the person's terminal can make a prompt appear even with a pipe on standard input. The typed-back confirmation is read from the terminal (`Prompt::line`), never from a pipe, and `klatch admin shell` refuses unless standard input is itself a terminal (checked before anyone is asked). `recover` (store key) and `restore` run before the store opens when it cannot be read, because the verifier is in it: `recover` is approved by the recovery code given (it must open the store file) and `restore` by the typed recovery code (constant time), each then followed by the typed confirmation; a readable store always uses the passphrase. `init-passphrase`'s check and store are one write transaction, the passphrase buffer is allocated to its maximum so it never reallocates and is wiped, and argon2 runs with its `zeroize` feature. Not fixed here, ticketed: a lockout by an agent with a pseudo-terminal of its own (#1274), the integrity of the verifier row (#1275, relates to #1261), the removed worker and agent-session refusals (#1276: the proof replaced them; a worker or agent that shares the person's terminal can still make a prompt appear), the macOS monotonic clock that stops in sleep (#1277), and a terminal left silent by SIGTERM during the prompt (#1278).

     Second review fixes (same item). Commands that print a secret (`recovery`, `export`, `run-archive`, `rotate-key`, `rotate-label-key`) refuse before any prompt when standard output is not a terminal, and `recovery` takes a typed confirmation. Store-less `recover` needs an existing encrypted store file that the code opens (`custody::opens_encrypted_store`), and the proofs by recovery code are built inside `klatch::proof` from the keystore and the store file, so nothing outside can build an `AdminProof` from a bare yes. The `restore` prompt names the backup file, every prompt names the store, and prompt text from arguments is stripped of control characters, escapes, bidi overrides and zero-width marks, never cuts the family and command words, cuts a long word with its length and a digest, and hides every non-flag word after `recover`. `prove` reads the verifier again once the attempt is typed and rewrites a weaker one only if the row is unchanged, in one transaction. Ticketed: `anchor github` payload (#1279), the open shell window on a shared terminal (#1280), the store-less recovery code limit (#1281), `worker issue` output (#1282), and the shared lockout now also noted on #1274.

     Third review fixes (same item). The stdout-must-be-a-terminal rule is now stdout-must-be-THIS-process's-controlling-terminal (`proof::is_controlling_tty`: a terminal, `tcgetpgrp` succeeds and `tcgetsid` is our session), because a pty an agent owns on fd 1 is a terminal too; it covers every act that prints stored text (the audit, refusals, review queue, scrub terms, archive listing as well as the secrets), and the admin shell needs fd 0 and 1 to be it. Prompt text from arguments is a whitelist (printable ASCII, everything else `?`), `recover` is hidden only when it is the command word and then every later word is, non-UTF-8 arguments are shown lossily, and the confirmation label says what it confirms. SECURITY.md states the residual: a passphrase typed into a terminal that a program hosts or relays is captured. Ticketed: the digest of a cut argument cannot be checked (#1283) and the rehash compare for non-canonical row text is untested (#1284).

     Fourth review fixes (same item). Being the controlling terminal is not enough for output: a program can lead a session on a pty of its own (forkpty), making that pty the controlling terminal of the child while standard input is the person's terminal, so the prompt appears on the person's terminal and the answer goes to the program. `proof::output_is_the_prompt_terminal` now also requires standard output to be the very device the prompt is read from and written to (`fstat` on both: device, inode, terminal), and standard error to be it or `/dev/null`; the shell needs fd 0 and 1 to be one controlling terminal. A recovery code is hidden by the PARSED command (`AdminCmd::Recover`), never by looking at words, so global flags before the subcommand or a flag value that is a family name cannot defeat it and a room named `recover` still shows. A confirmation label for a name that had to be cleaned shows its length and the digest of the real name. SECURITY.md no longer claims `setsid` is refused: it is not, and says exactly what the same-device check does and does not do.

306. **The SemVer to PEP 440 mapping is one script, and check-versions.sh checks the Homebrew formula (#1234, #1235; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** **Question.** The first release is 0.2.0-alpha.1 (PyPI 0.2.0a1, tag v0.2.0a1), but only `-dev.N` was mapped, in five copies (check-versions.sh, install.sh and three checks in build-release.yml). How do alpha, beta, rc and dev share one rule so the sites cannot drift, and how does a version bump stop leaving the Homebrew formula behind?

   **Decision.** `release/pep440.sh` is the one definition, POSIX sh, sourced: `semver_to_pep440`, `pep440_to_semver`, `release_tag`, `semver_of_tag`, `tag_matches`, `archive_name`, `is_prerelease`. The table is `-alpha.N` to `aN`, `-beta.N` to `bN`, `-rc.N` to `rcN`, `-dev.N` to `.devN`, a plain X.Y.Z unchanged; anything else is refused with a non-zero status rather than passed through, because a pass-through is how five copies drifted. `check-versions.sh` and the three tag checks in build-release.yml source it (each of those lines became one line that calls `tag_matches`, which accepts the PEP 440 tag and the SemVer spelling as before). `install/install.sh` is downloaded alone and cannot source a file, so it carries a copy of the marked block; `release/test-pep440.sh` fails when the copy and the source differ, and when another script spells the mapping again. check-versions.sh now requires PyPI and the wheel to equal the PEP 440 spelling of the crate version exactly (before, either spelling passed).

   The formula lives in the tap `hslee16/homebrew-klatch`; a staged copy is in `release/homebrew/klatch.rb`. check-versions.sh reads it (or a path argument) and checks `version`, that there is exactly one URL per platform archive and that each is `https://github.com/hslee16/klatch/releases/download/<tag>/klatch-<semver>-<target>.tar.gz`, and the SHA-256 lines when present (64 hex digits). With a SHA256SUMS file as the third argument every archive's sha256 must match its line, and the formula must carry the lines.

   **Alternatives.** Sourcing pep440.sh from install.sh at run time (the installer is read, checksummed and run alone, so it cannot). Generating install.sh from a template (a build step for a file people read before running; the sync test is cheaper). A mapping table in a data file read by sed (still needs a reader in every site).

   **Ordering.** Never `0.1.0-alpha.1`: SemVer compares labels as text, so it sorts below the published `0.1.0-dev.2`. `0.2.0-alpha.1` sorts above every `0.1.0-*` in both schemes (tested). SemVer and PEP 440 disagree about `dev` against `alpha` on the same X.Y.Z, so one X.Y.Z line uses one kind.

   **Not covered.** The staged formula is a copy; nothing compares it with the tap's file (the bump script ticket owns the copy to the tap). The wheel job in build-release.yml compares the tag with PyPI's spelling by plain equality, not through the mapping. The real version numbers are not bumped here (#1248).

307. **klatch-server ships as the second binary of every release archive, the platform wheels and the Homebrew formula (#1236; decision D1 of the release epic #1232; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** Before this, `klatch-server` had `publish = false`, no `--version`, and was in no archive, wheel or formula, so the MVP scope (the daemon over a Unix socket, loopback HTTP and MCP, the webhook intake) could not be installed. Decision D1 (human, 2026-11-21): it ships next to `klatch`. The pieces: (1) `release/package-archive.sh` builds each archive with `klatch`, `klatch-server`, LICENSE and THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt under one directory and refuses to build one without both binaries; `release/check-archive.sh` asserts that exact layout (and that both are executable) and, with `--smoke`, unpacks the archive and runs `release/check-packaged-server.sh` on the server in it. build-release.yml calls both for each of the six targets, so what CI packs is what `release/test-package-archive.sh` tests (with stand-in binaries, no build). (2) The two binaries are built by two `cargo build` invocations, not one, so the `klatch` binary is exactly what `cargo build -p klatch` makes and `klatch-server` stays its own artifact. (3) One CycloneDX 1.5 SBOM per archive covers both: cargo-cyclonedx runs once per binary and `release/merge-sbom.py` merges the two (klatch's metadata, klatch-server as a component, every component and dependency once by bom-ref); the workflow fails if `klatch-server`, `subtle` or `hmac` is missing from the result. (4) The notices need no new config: cargo-about and cargo-deny already run over the whole workspace with all features, so klatch-server's own crates (libc, sha2, subtle, hmac) were already in THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt and under the deny policy; `proto/check-notices.sh` now also fails if klatch-server itself or any crate in the dependency tree of either binary is missing from the file, so this stays true. (5) The platform wheels put `klatch-server` in `<name>.data/scripts/` beside `klatch`, so pip installs both on the environment's PATH; `build_wheels.py` takes the second binary from the same archive, checks the macOS minimum of both, and refuses an archive without it; the workflow asserts the contents of every wheel (both scripts, executable, both licence files). The npm package is the TypeScript client and never held a binary, so it gets nothing. (6) `install/install.sh` extracts the one archive whose SHA-256 it verified (it covers both binaries), refuses it if either binary is missing, writes both beside their targets and renames only when both are written, so an existing install is replaced whole or not at all; UNINSTALL.md removes both. (7) The Homebrew formula lives in the tap repository, not here: RELEASING.md now says the bump must install and test both binaries, and the tap change is ticketed.

       The claim. `klatch-server` does open sockets, so "no telemetry, no outbound calls" (true of `klatch`, pinned by `proto/klatch/tests/no_telemetry.rs`) is not reused for it. SECURITY.md and the README state a second claim: klatch-server opens loopback listeners only, starts no program and makes no outbound connection. It is backed by `proto/klatch-server/tests/no_network.rs` (dependency tree and source audit, the one `TcpListener::bind` after the loopback check, the daemon's single Unix listener and probe), now also by a test that the optional `jev-http` transport (ureq, rustls, ring, webpki-roots) is not in its build, a test that both documents state the claim, and, on the packaged binary of every archive, `release/check-packaged-server.sh`: `--version` prints `klatch-server <version>` (a new flag; `-V` too), `--help` names the subcommands, `http --listen 0.0.0.0:0` and `[::]:0` are refused with a refusal message, and `daemon --socket` in a throwaway home starts, makes its socket, stops on SIGTERM and removes it. Like the audit for `klatch`, this is a check of what the code could do and of three behaviours, not a capture of one run's traffic.

       Crates.io. The recommendation, taken: `klatch-server` stays unpublished (`publish = false`; `no_network.rs` pins it). Reasons: `cargo install klatch` keeps installing only the network-free binary, so nobody gets a socket-opening program by accident through the crate named in the README; the binary reaches users through the archives (checksummed, signed, attested, with the SBOM), the wheels and Homebrew, which is where the checks above run; a second published crate would need `klatch = { path, version }`, its own `cargo publish --dry-run --locked` in CI and the gate, a lockstep entry in `release/check-versions.sh`, and a trusted-publishing entry on crates.io, all for a path nobody asked for. A user who wants it from source runs `cargo install --locked --git https://github.com/hslee16/klatch klatch-server`. Publishing it later is a decision that removes `publish = false`, changes the `no_network.rs` pin and adds the dry run.

       What is not covered here: the real six-target build and the SBOM merge on real cargo-cyclonedx output are only exercised by the Release dry run workflow (manual); this change was checked locally for the host target (release build, the archive made by the same script, its listing, the packaged-server check on the real binary, a wheel built from the archive). The musl and Intel targets are built and smoke tested by that workflow, as before.

308. **The first release documents say local-only, not 1.0, and not for sensitive data (#1237, #1238; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** The human decided on 2026-11-21 that the first release is a public local-only pre-release, a scoped MVP and not a 1.0 (epic #1232), after the decision of 2026-11-14 (#419) that marked 0.x pre-releases ship and the gate of #152 applies to the first unmarked release. The README, `SECURITY.md`, `UNINSTALL.md`, `INTEGRATION.md` and the top of DESIGN.md and section 23 now say that, and drop the sentences from before the daemon and the loopback listeners existed (the claims of no daemon, no network transport, an unbuilt system, a plaintext store and an unpublished project).

   **The wire statement.** Remote transports are not part of this release. On one machine klatch uses stdio, a Unix socket and, for tools that need it, loopback HTTP with a bearer credential; none of these is encrypted, none leaves the machine, and a non-loopback listener refuses to start. The second binding maxim is met as section 32 item 6 defines it for local use (operating system credentials are enough locally). Loopback HTTP is the weakest local route: prefer the Unix socket or stdio, never forward or proxy the port, and reach it from another machine only through ssh or mutual TLS (`clients/recipes/behind-a-tunnel.md`).

   **Two claims, not one.** The `klatch` binary opens no socket and starts only the programs `no_telemetry.rs` names. `klatch-server`, the second binary, opens loopback listeners and a Unix socket only, starts no program and makes no outbound connection (`no_network.rs`). The documents keep the two apart so the shorter claim never covers the server.

   **The gate is worded as "no unmarked release".** DESIGN section 23, R40 and the header say so, matching #419; the body of #152 and the milestone say the same.

   **Pinned by tests.** `proto/klatch/tests/release_scope_docs.rs` fails if a stale phrase returns, or if the scope sentence, the wire statement, the platform split (tested: macOS arm64 and Linux x86_64; built and smoke tested only: Linux aarch64 and macOS x86_64), the known limits or the klatch-server uninstall steps are dropped.

309. **Release steps are scripts with fake-backed tests (#1239, #1240, #1241, #1242, #1249; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** release/rehearse-assemble.sh runs the changelog, DESIGN and RESEARCH assembly and check-versions.sh on a throwaway copy and prints the diff stat and the references that still need renumbering; release/release-notes.sh fills RELEASE-NOTES.template.md from SECURITY.md "What is not protected" and the open audit gap rows; release/bump-formula.sh rewrites the tap formula from SHA256SUMS and prints a patch and REST commands but never pushes; release/rollback-rehearsal.sh prints the yank, deprecate and revert commands and runs the reversible ones only with --execute, tokens in the environment and a pre-release version; release/verify-published.sh checks PyPI, npm, crates.io, the GitHub release, the tap formula and a clean install from the public side.

   Every script is bash 3.2 plus python3 standard library, runs no network call in its test (fake gh, curl, npm, pip, brew), prints no token and has no sleep. The scripts that could publish (bump-formula, rollback-rehearsal) are print-only by default and never call gh or git themselves except the explicit --execute of the rollback rehearsal. RELEASING.md describes each in its section instead of repeating the steps.

310. **Package contents are asserted by one script (#1243; release tooling only; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** release/check-package-contents.sh takes a wheel or an npm tarball. A wheel must hold the klatch and klatch-server commands (both executable), LICENSE and THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt; an npm tarball must hold dist/, README.md, LICENSE and package.json and nothing else, so no binary. build-release.yml calls it for the wheels (replacing the inline check of #1236) and, new here, for the npm tarball after npm pack, both inside existing jobs. The human can run it on downloaded artifacts. release/test-check-package-contents.sh checks it on fixture wheels and tarballs (a good one, one missing a file, a non-executable server, a binary or stray file in npm) and runs in the CI scripts step. Release archives stay checked by release/check-archive.sh.

311. **Renumbering the references after assembly (#1244; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** After `design-fragments.sh assemble` and `research-fragments.sh assemble` the fragments have numbers, but text across the tree still says "the design item for #T" or "the research note for #T". `release/renumber-refs.sh MAPFILE [root]` rewrites them from the summary lines the two assemble steps print on stderr (`item N <- #T (file)` and `section N <- #T (file)`; other lines are ignored). **Form:** "design item for #T" becomes "design item N"; "research note for #T" becomes "research note section N". **Scope:** every text file under the root except `.git`, `target`, `node_modules`, `release/` (its tests carry fixtures) and `*.jsonl` (data such as the judge corpus, whose text must not change); the literal placeholder "#NNN" is never a reference. **Loud failure:** the whole tree is scanned first; if any referenced ticket has no number in the map (for example a reference to an item that is not a fragment), the tickets are named on stderr, the exit status is 1 and nothing is changed. **Output:** one line per change, `file:line: old -> new`. A second run finds nothing and exits 0. **In the rehearsal:** `release/rehearse-assemble.sh` captures the assemble summaries, runs the renumbering on the copy before the changelog collect (so the entries carry the numbers), and reports zero remaining placeholders; on the tree at the time of writing it renumbers the references with none left over. **Tests:** `release/test-renumber-refs.sh` (fixture with design and research references, exclusions, a placeholder, a second run, a reference with no item that must fail and change nothing, a missing map) and the extended `release/test-rehearse-assemble.sh`; both were written before the script and failed first because it did not exist. The gate runs the new test.

312. **Hooks that `klatch init` installs now carry an explicit 30 second timeout, so the first Keychain read after a migration (bounded at 5 seconds) can fail with its message before Claude Code kills the hook (#1246; 2026-11-21).** The Claude Code default hook timeout was not verified (#372 left it open), so the entry no longer relies on it. `init` writes `"timeout": 30` on the SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PostToolUse, SessionEnd and the optional claim hook, adds it to an older entry that has none, raises a lower one, and keeps a longer one a user chose. The wake hook keeps its 3600 s. The Codex hook file is unchanged. The 5 second limit is now the shared constant `KEYCHAIN_READ_LIMIT_SECS` in `keys`, and a test requires every installed entry to exceed it. The Keychain read message already says what to do (answer the dialog with Always Allow, or run the build that stored the key). The result is recorded on #372.

313. **Manual platform test workflow (#1247; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** `.github/workflows/platform-tests.yml` is `workflow_dispatch` only (no schedule, no pull request trigger, so CI stays light) and runs `cargo test --workspace --locked` on ubuntu-22.04-arm and macos-15-intel with a 60 minute timeout. `release/check-timeouts.sh` now covers it. The README platform table keeps "built and smoke tested" for those two rows until a manual run is green; the table says "tested" only for what a run proved. Follow-up (#1247): run 44978689847 passed on both platforms, so the README, SECURITY.md and release notes template now say "tested" for them.

314. **The release workflow checks tags through the one mapping, and the tap formula is compared with the staged copy (#1260; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** Every tag check in `.github/workflows/build-release.yml` now goes through `release/pep440.sh` (`tag_matches`), so a tag in either spelling of a release (v0.2.0a1 or v0.2.0-alpha.1) passes all four jobs and any other tag fails them. Before, the two Python steps compared the tag with the PyPI version by plain equality. The wheel job also refuses a Python version that is not the same release as the crate (the wheels are named by the PyPI version, the archives by the crate version), which nothing checked. `release/test-tag-checks.sh` reads the real steps out of the workflow file and runs them against fixture version files for every kind of release, so what is tested is what CI runs.

   The staged formula (`release/homebrew/klatch.rb`) and the tap's formula can no longer drift unseen. `release/compare-formula.sh` fails with a diff on any difference beyond the version, the URLs and the hashes. `release/bump-formula.sh` runs it against the tap checkout before it rewrites anything (so a drifted tap or staged copy stops the bump), and writes the bumped formula back to the staged copy, which is then committed with the release. `release/verify-published.sh` reads the tap formula over REST (`gh api`) and runs the same comparison as its `tap-drift` check. The dry-run workflow itself was not run from this session; the tag steps were run through their real script bodies instead.

315. **The assembly rehearsal can also run the checks that assembling breaks (#1286; release tooling only; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** Assembling the fragments into DESIGN.md broke four things the rehearsal did not run: a test that read a design fragment by file name, DESIGN items quoting phrases that `release_scope_docs` forbids, a script test that pinned the old version, and `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`, which carries the workspace version. `release/rehearse-assemble.sh --check-tests` now runs, on the throwaway copy after the assembly, a version check of the notices file (the cheap part of `proto/check-notices.sh`, which needs cargo-about), `cargo test -p klatch --test release_scope_docs`, `cargo test -p klatch --lib negotiate` and every `release/test-*.sh` except the rehearsal's own test. The flag is optional so the default rehearsal stays fast. `REHEARSE_CARGO` and `REHEARSE_TARGET_DIR` override cargo and its target directory (default: the real tree's `proto/target`, so there is no cold build); the tests use a fake cargo. RELEASING.md section 1 describes the flag.

316. **The staged formula may be ahead of the tap by exactly the klatch-server lines before a release, and never after (#1287; no schema change; 2026-11-21).** `release/compare-formula.sh` takes one named option, `--allow-klatch-server`. With it, the staged formula (the release candidate) may differ from the tap formula (the previous release) by exactly two lines besides the version, URLs and hashes: `bin.install "klatch", "klatch-server"` in place of `bin.install "klatch"`, and the one test line `assert_match "usage: klatch-server", shell_output("#{bin}/klatch-server --help")`. Each may be undone once, as an exact line; any other extra, changed or duplicated line is still drift, and a tap that is ahead of the staged copy is drift. `release/bump-formula.sh` passes the option, because on release day the tap formula (0.1.0-dev.2) predates klatch-server and the bump is what adds the lines to it.

   `release/verify-published.sh` does not pass the option: after the release the published tap formula and the staged copy (which the bump replaced with the bumped formula) must be equal apart from the version, URLs and hashes, so a tap that lacks the klatch-server lines fails `tap-drift`. The allowance is therefore a before-release rule only, in the one script that writes the lines, and the comparison with no allowance is the proof that the tap really carries them.

317. **The bench follows the admin passphrase proof (#1288; bench only; no product, schema or wire change; 2026-11-21).** After #1271 two bench scenarios failed. `admin_controls_pause_close_remove_and_audit` expected the old audit order; every gated admin act now writes a `proof-ok` row, and the first passphrase set writes `passphrase-set`, so the expected list was updated (the refused pause with a wrong confirmation also proved the passphrase, hence the extra `proof-ok`). `the_store_is_encrypted_and_a_lost_key_comes_back_from_its_recovery_code` typed the admin passphrase into `admin recover`; with the key lost the store cannot be read, so recover asks only for the typed confirmation, and the passphrase text was taken as a wrong confirmation. This is a bench adaptation, not a regression: proto/klatch/tests/admin_proof.rs (`recover_works_when_the_store_key_is_lost`) drives the same path at a pty and passes. The bench gained `World::admin_keyless` for commands that run before the store opens.
